## 2026-11-06, Implement approved subtask curriculum and receiver-focused observations
**What was decided:** The user approves the prior recommendation. Implement subtask-loose resets using the shared v10 qualified predecessor scenes, keep original all-loose banks separate, preserve per-entry SGS, and build balanced groups targeting 256 TRAIN / 64 EVAL each (6,400 / 1,600 total). Add a resumable, fail-closed generator and versioned observation layout with dense receiver/prerequisite/tool coverage. Change training/launcher defaults to the dedicated subtask bank, leaving full-sequence reset generation and Quest scene initialization separate. Policy training is not launched by this authorization.
**Why:** Later subtasks should learn the requested placement with completed prerequisites; the old all-loose family required an entire assembly sequence. Existing v10 completed scenes pass native qualification, allowing removal of the old retainer workaround. Fixed point-budget reallocation changes input semantics even at the same tensor shape, so checkpoint compatibility must bind the observation contract.
**What was rejected:** Silently loading old weights with changed points, increasing state counts through duplicates, copying v9 receipts onto v10 states, publishing missing/imbalanced families, weakening physical gates, or changing the SGS weighting rule or task reward without need. Implementation and pilot generation are in progress; current evidence lives in `artifacts_local/subtask-curriculum-v10/`. The first pilot found an invalid generic horn-near grip after the v10 rotation change; the second uses qualified measured-grasp placement with coupled arm/receiver fitting and preserves unchanged gates. Its adoption mechanism reuses already-qualified rows only under matching physical/validator identities. The old generated `artifacts_local/motor-family-review/pickup.mjb` was losslessly archived as `pickup.mjb.gz` after verifying the decompressed SHA256 `cc2fea246c737dd217f957213bc04015bbc67046eaa044e9f7f6eac07ee95c8b`, saving 278,539,774 bytes; decompress before rerunning an old exporter that expects the uncompressed path.

## 2026-11-06, Review staged loose starts and SGS bank scale
**What was decided:** Treat this turn as design review, preserving runtime/reset/sampler code. Recommend subtask-specific loose starts with physically completed prerequisites, denser receiving-part/tool observations, balanced initial bank counts and subsequent expansion to 6,400 then 16,000 TRAIN states. Implementation remains pending user agreement; all-loose full assembly should have a separate distribution and evaluation.
**Why:** Current defaults supply 128 active-part points, 128 context points split over ten other bodies (12–13 each), and 64 gripper points. The other parts are present in observations, but later all-loose starts require prerequisite construction within the same 15 s sparse-reward episode. ResetSampler operates on individual pooled entries, with no task/family equalization: the v9 bank's 160/287 loose entries get 55.75% of initial reset probability. The SGS paper uses 32,768 configurations for each single-task manipulation setup; AssembleNIST's public defaults instead use 640 × 5 families × 20 tasks = 64,000 TRAIN entries. The source is now v10-mating, so prior v9 pose validation is historical until revalidated.
**What was rejected:** Claiming prerequisites are absent from observations, treating valid poses as sufficient curriculum design, assuming SGS balances family labels or creates new states, counting EVAL entries as TRAIN capacity, copying thousands of nearly identical poses to inflate counts, or silently implementing a new observation/reset/sampling design from a question.
**Sources:** Local `so101/obs.py`, `rl/sampler.py`, `train.py`; https://arxiv.org/html/2610.12465v1#A2 ; https://arxiv.org/html/2610.13354v1#S4.SS2 ; https://github.com/suniique/assembly-nist/blob/main/scripts/build_banks.py .

## 2026-11-05, H100 command includes task learning assessment
**What was decided:** Provide `scripts/cloud/test_learning_h100.sh` using one allocated GPU, CPU-affinity/Slurm-aware threads, a 512–4096-world starting size, four-hour budget and explicit overrides. ## 2026-11-05, Focus on motor insertion direction and force
**User focus:** Start with the base/motor and compare horizontal sliding with insertion from above for applying force. Preserve the six-stage scope; this is the immediate debugging priority.

## 2026-11-06, Paper-based point sampling and runtime task roles
**User request and scope:** Search the paper, recommend more points in smarter regions, check whether base/gripper placement is correct and whether the active part is overallocated, and explain active-role switching while running. This authorizes research, read-only diagnostics and a concrete proposal; no production sampler, scene, learner weights or live service is changed.
**Primary evidence:** Re-read AlphaNIST arXiv:2610.13354v1 sections III-A/IV-B/IV-D/V-D and Figure 3, plus the independent upstream AssembleNIST sampler. Paper inputs are movable/mating/robot geometry without a discrete task label; Figure 3's 128 is an encoder width, not a verified point budget. The independent code's 128 part/128 mating/64 gripper defaults are not a paper-proven optimum. Our adapter spreads the mating budget across other bodies. Current v8 has five tasks and 13 base points in the motor table; the existing v6 visualization remains historical. SGS selects reset states/tasks, not surface points. The paper's multi-pair policies are separately trained and sequence without an explicit planner, unlike our single-task adapter.
**Native verification:** On frozen live state with matching v8 identity and a separate asymmetric shoulder/jaw configuration, all 640 tested samples lie on the corresponding native surface within 0.000056 mm; base errors are below 0.000015 mm. No rigid-transform defect found in those cases. Palm versus named TCP site differs by 98.445 mm. This does not establish real-kit calibration or region coverage. Initial metre-scale trimesh queries falsely flagged tiny CAD triangles; retain that failed audit and corrected millimetre-query receipt. Production sample coordinates were not changed.
**Proposed experiment, not selected implementation:** 512 samples: 96 moving part, 192 receiving/mating geometry, 128 grippers (32 each body) and 96 context/tool. Reserve functional cavity/rim/pad samples plus broad shape, use deterministic offline region-constrained FPS and fixed ordered tables. Compare current320, smart320 (60/120/80/60) and smart512 on identical validated starts/budgets. PointNet++ supports coverage-oriented FPS, not claimed assembly improvement. Changing even equal-sized feature allocation requires an observation schema/version and compatible new training; 512 changes the encoder layer shape. Do not resample/reorder dynamically under the existing flattened MLP.
**Runtime distinction:** Current training changes task through reset entries after episode termination; policy_demo runs one fixed task and stops. Quest A loads prepared targets, not learned sequential continuation. A proposed coordinator would validate current completion, change active/receiver tables and task validator, preserve physical state, simulation clock, previous actions and real history, and reset only stage-specific counters. Do not use set_states/prepared loading to simulate a continuous handoff. Train on actual predecessor handoffs before claiming sequencing; autonomous order requires a different joint objective/observation experiment.
**Session end:** Completed source research, native diagnostics, recommendation and intent/task/input-guide updates. Original live Quest and production input remain unchanged; no restart, commit/push or new training. Next if selected: implement versioned candidate samples and a fresh comparison view, measure coverage, then learning/safety and compute.
